# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
scripts/test.sh                      # everything blocking (60 s budget); reports every failure, never fail-fast
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
# Linux qualification host: strace >=6.8 on PATH (audits every successful TCP listen).
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.
---
name: llp-orient
description: Before editing a subsystem, file, or starting a task, assemble the governing LLP context — read the root LLP, follow the @ref annotations on the code in scope, and find the LLPs covering the relevant systems. Produces a compact context pack of the constraints a change must respect. Read-only.
source: ccheever/llp@v0.5.2
---

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

## 16. SEC7: advice that reaches the builder

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22. Wave-22 commission;
DX6 owns session/upgrade/clock and frontend/proof-kit fixes. No version bump.
This section supersedes §15.2's special display cap for insert/result notes and
§12.3's advisory-only boundary only for the two explicit gates below.
It also revises §§13.5/14.2/14.5/14.6: disposition-clearable review gates are
a new exit-1 category alongside replayed intent violations, including without
invariants. Their zero-new-refusal bars continue to bind failures no disposition
can clear (compilation failures and reachable refutations). Gate counts and
legitimate review costs are reported separately, never called replayed exploits. Runtime
authority and the proved/refuted/unproved contract remain unchanged.

### 16.1 Presentation and conditional questions

A shared severity ordering puts gated notes and likely-hole questions before
coverage, restates and result inventories. Default check retains every priority
0–2 likely-hole question and the first five ordinary code classes in severity
order, keeping each selected class's distinct sites/audiences; SEC6 insert/result
notes never increase that budget. Result exposure notes group by source table, retaining every operation,
column, observer kind and source site in JSON/full text. Audit uses the same
severity and numeric file/line/column ordering, never lexicographic site strings.
Insert-write advice requires an explicitly supplied nonliteral expression whose
flow uses a mutation argument; omitted defaults and literal-only assignments are
quiet. These are precision changes to advice, never creation-policy inference.

New advisory classes ask about: reason text without a positive non-whitespace
minimum; optional money supplied to creation; constant true payment/approval
flags (a naming heuristic, explicitly a question); competing generic enum setters
versus stricter literal transitions; mutable creation checks omitted by a later
transition or derived creation; inconsistent server-now duration bounds on the
same bound column across operations; and global-role operations recording viewer
on an unrelated row. Restating questions and the draft ask whether each role may
write any row, independently of the read questionnaire. The relationship recipe
models the owner of this wall explicitly. These shapes cannot infer the spec.

### 16.2 Narrow gates and individual dispositions

N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE: an optional image/file mutation argument flows to an
insert/upsert of a media column, including a conditional child insertion. A
non-null guard around just the child does not establish required parent evidence.
An unconditional dominating require of non-null before any creating write can
exclude the note; aliases/helpers are resolved by the existing authority walker.
Optionality may be intentional: the gate requires review, never asserts an exploit.

N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT: a read branch trusts a retained grant; an authored grant
writer requires a literal role on the principal argument stored as that grant's
reader principal; that role is mutable through authored operations; and the read
branch does not imply the same current-reader role. Require a positive recipient
role atom in every admitting writer branch; do not mistake the assigner's role
for the recipient's. The gate excludes ordinary owner branches, literal service
seats and mere caller-role requirements. Revoking derived grants is a repair;
static inability to prove full revocation remains reviewable, not an automatic
safety theorem. General N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE stays advisory.

Unreviewed gates make check exit 1 with structured note IDs, without marking an
invariant refuted. The existing bulk --ack-notes cannot clear a security gate.
Use check --ack-security <id> --reason '<spec rationale>' for one current note.
Store versioned dispositions in snapback/security-reviews.json with ID, reason,
source digest and analyzer revision. IDs hash the complete diagnostic; the source
digest hashes all compiled source filenames and bytes in sorted order. Every
source edit, changed diagnostic or analyzer revision invalidates the disposition.
Blank reasons, unknown IDs, malformed files and incompatible flag combinations
refuse. Writes use temp+rename and verify compiled sources still match disk;
failed compilation/refuted intent must never record acknowledgments. Dispositions
are review records, never authority or proof, remain visible in JSON/audit and
can be checked into source control. Broad advice may also be individually reviewed.

### 16.3 Directed finite histories

Keep the 512 breadth/pair budget. Add a separate bounded directed frontier before
breadth exploration for schema enum transitions and mutable dependencies of
independent intent/creator checks. Generate successors from the current world,
so references returned/created at one step are available later. Track semantic
state signatures over status/role/boolean/relationship fields, rather than minted
IDs alone; prioritize unseen lifecycle signatures and schema-relevant mutations.
Bound depth to eight authored mutations, attempts to 2048 and retained frontier
to 16 worlds; share check's existing work/deadline cap and report every cut.
Actual mutation admission, executed-write judgments and ordinary observers remain
the only failure oracle. Synthetic witnesses remain REVIEW and never failures.
Validated generated initial fixtures retain their explicit provenance. No seed
state is relabeled application reachable, and finite exhaustion remains unproved.

### 16.4 Qualification

Regression tests cover positive and negative shapes, helper/alias guards,
per-note/source/analyzer invalidation, failed-check non-writing, severity and
numeric ordering, and lifecycle/suspension histories beyond pairs. Measure every
wave-22 schema's default visibility and waves 15,16,18–22's gate counts separately
from false refusals. Optional evidence can legitimately fail pending a disposition;
report that cost explicitly rather than claim every gate is a confirmed hole.
Re-run copies of the eight high lanes without intent, with authored intent, and
with one independent spec-worded invariant. Advice is flagged; only a replayed
violation is caught. Packed qualification executes every changed guide snippet.

### 16.5 Review clarification: exact detector and command boundaries

These details resolve design review M1–M6. The implementation is the bounded
shape detector, not an inference that a schema satisfies its product specification.

Priority codes are fixed by security::note_priority: gates at 0; privilege and
self-claimed relationship at 1; grant-outlives, optional evidence, role-wide read,
broad read, caller time, sweep deadline and the seven new question codes at 2;
ordinary notes at 4; insert/result inventory at 5; restates at 6; intent coverage
at 7. The short list keeps every action at 0–2 plus the first five ordinary
code classes, preserving all sites/audiences within those classes. Repeated sites
of one ordinary code do not consume independent classes; insert/result codes
never enlarge the budget. This avoids hiding a second scan audience or a distinct
maintain diagnostic behind repeated bootstrap notes. Concrete
reachable refutations still print all failing sites, origins and repair commands
outside this list. A remainder count points to verbose/JSON. JSON retains every
note. Restates and coverage can appear only after the action classes fit.

Creation reason: an argument flows into an inserted/upserted column with `reason`
in its case-insensitive name and its text argument minimum is absent or zero.
Optional money: the optional argument flows into an optional Money column, or an
Int/IntRange/Decimal column whose lowercased name contains price, charge, fee or
amount. Constant approval: true literal assigned to a creating column containing
paid, approv or payment. All are name-based specification questions; no claim
covers other spellings. Required positive-minimum reasons and required money
arguments suppress their respective advice. One note per operation/column.

Transition bypass: same-table enum update from an enum argument can choose a
literal also written by another mutation, and its necessary effective admission
does not imply that other writer's bound requirements plus table gate. Resolve
row-local read operands to the target table's focal row for this advisory query.
A dominating contradictory literal argument guard excludes that target. Generic
setter/table/target/stricter-op duplicates collapse by exact diagnostic message.
A stricter-gate failure alone is advisory, never an inferred approval violation.
Stale creation: a resolved creating-write context reads a non-role dependency
that another mutation updates/upserts/deletes, and a different later mutation
updates the created table or writes a referencing child without reading that
dependency in its dominating context. Group by operation, created table and
dependency. Mere dependency reads do not prove eligibility; this heuristic's
false negatives and speculative questions are not gated.
Duration: compare the same resolved table/column against now +/- literal duration
with the same inequality direction in two operation contexts. Different offsets
produce one question listing all windows/operations. Other affine expressions,
custom wrappers and complex derived windows remain uncovered by this advice.
Role-wide write: a mutation updates a non-immutable Principal to viewer, has a
bound global role requirement, and lacks a dominating viewer-to-row-member
relationship comparison. It asks about this resource rather than declaring an
unauthorized write. The draft is bounded by discovered schema roles × tables;
it asks every pair about write authority, including pairs with authored intent.

Optional upload: the existing lexical authority walker resolves aliases and
helper arguments to the original mutation argument. Non-null suppression accepts
only a direct positive `argument != null` conjunct in an unconditional Require
before the first creating-write context. `$advice` branch guards cannot suppress;
late child guards, OR, unknown and unsupported expressions cannot suppress.
Known flows into asset fields are detected; arbitrary opaque/derived flows are
uncovered, never assumed safe. Both upsert arms retain the creation review question.
Retained role: use existing lints::mutable_role and privilege role-column/probe
resolution. Only direct positive Exists conjuncts with a literal recipient role
qualify, never an assigner/viewer-only test or negation. A branch suppresses only
when kernel::implies(branch, recipient-role-with-viewer) is Ok(true); false,
unknown and cap exhaustion retain the question. Dynamic role requirements stay
in general grant advice and are outside the narrow gate. Every noted branch
contains a positive grant probe binding that grant's principal column to viewer.
Owner equalities without such probes are excluded structurally. Role mutation
plus transactional grant revocation still needs review; it is not statically
proved safe or automatically refused as an exploit.

Disposition format is version 1: entries maps full diagnostic SHA256 IDs to
{sources, analyzer, reason}. JSON uses the existing Diagnostic serialization and
the sorted Vec<(filename, UTF-8 source bytes)> serialization used by Project.sources;
source names are schema-directory-relative (`name.q`). A line shift changes the
ID; any source edit changes all dispositions. This intentionally conservative
invalidation avoids unmodeled cross-table influence; optimization is deferred.
Analyzer is the explicit security_reviews::ANALYZER revision, bumped whenever
security detector meaning or disposition identity changes. A release alone does
not invalidate unchanged analysis. A reason must contain non-whitespace text.
No unrelated refuted invariant can be bypassed: any reachable refutation prevents
all acknowledgement writes and check still exits 1. Compilation errors likewise
write no security review. Read-only inspection remains available. Bulk --ack-notes
never addresses these records. --ack-security requires --reason and refuses
combination with --ack-notes, --fix, --store, --release, --emit-lean or --audit;
--json, --verbose, --cost and current-source fixture/residency inspection may
combine. Broad-advice dispositions change display, never exit status. Audit exits
on refuted intent as before and includes all notes/dispositions; new gates fail
check only. Deploy/preview/--strict keep §15.3; we recommend running check in acceptance before
deploy. Saved-source portability is not claimed for dispositions.

Directed exploration is primarily a test campaign: check retains the common
512-work/1500ms cap and may cut before it. No promise grants both directed and
breadth paths their full caps under check. Test retains its 512 singles/pairs
in addition to the directed bound. Successors fork the current complete fixture
through Engine::fork_fixture with its asset ledger; they use ordinary mutate,
NOW and existing executed/observer judgments. The signature keeps enum/bool/
principal/reference fields (with multiplicity), discarding other payload only as
a pruning heuristic. Such mergers and every frontier/argument/successor/depth cut
are explicitly uncovered. Retain up to four priority worlds (below the 16 maximum),
prefer changed enum states and true booleans, at most two new worlds per op/world,
four argument tuples, eight mutations and 2048 actual attempts. These finite
heuristics neither prove reachability nor clear an unproved verdict. Synthetic
matrix probes stay REVIEW; ordinary admitted histories from validated generated
initial fixtures retain GeneratedFixtureTrace and failure eligibility (§14).

Wave-22 high lanes are 004,011,021,026,030,037,040,044. Frozen source copies and
independent spec variants are in tests/security/wave22; the measurement manifest
records SHA256s. 037 remains the specification judgment case. With no invariants,
expect corresponding new/advisory notes on the seven nonjudgment lanes. Spec tests
must catch 026's terminal pickup and 030's suspend-before-approval history, alongside
existing 004/021/037 catches; 040/044 always/absent-money limits stay explicit.
Gate calibration targets: 021 for retained role; 003,018,027,040 for optional
uploads on wave22. 027 may be legitimate optionality: gate cost is review pending,
not a confirmed hole. Across waves15,16,18–22 report every project that fails a gate,
all compilation/refutation refusals separately, and every legitimate gate needing
a disposition. Zero false *unacknowledgeable* refusals is the acceptance criterion;
never relabel legitimate review gates zero false positives. Changed executable
guide material: wall-ownership Quarry in rule-recipes; existing cheatsheet,
security and upload-placement snippets are rerun from the fresh packed client.

### 16.6 Second-review resolutions

The two new gates are check-CI-only review obligations. Deploy still lists all
security notes in §15.3 review; its --strict boundary remains replayed refutations.
Running check in deployment acceptance is recommended, not an enforced fresh-check
receipt protocol. No saved-source deployment claim is made for these dispositions.
Acknowledged gates plus no other errors/refutations give exit 0. Default output
replaces each acknowledged note with its ID/code/reason review record; verbose,
JSON and audit retain the full note. Broad dispositions have this same display
effect with no exit-code effect. Stale entries remain in the file for review
history but do not acknowledge any current note. `check --json`, `check --verbose`
and audit inspect current notes without writing a disposition; malformed files
refuse with repair text. There is no wildcard or bulk security acknowledgment.

IDs use deterministic recursively key-sorted JSON diagnostic values; source digest
uses sorted source pairs. Tests require same-input/same-ID stability on repeated
checks in addition to invalidation controls. The analyzer revision is a reviewed
constant; changes to detector semantics or fingerprint serialization must bump it
and their tests must reject a previous revision. It is not a runtime security
boundary or promise that arbitrary modified binaries honor review semantics.

Transition advice emits only on definite Ok(false) implication, suppressing unknown
or capped comparisons as uncovered by that heuristic. Stale-creation, reason,
money, constant-approval and duration advice use finite structural matches only:
unknown/unsupported syntax does not match and is not inferred safe. Their documented
naming/type/binding fragment is their entire coverage, without a proof claim.
General retained-grant advice and the narrow gate may co-fire intentionally:
count gate projects by gated code and project, not total note count. Neither
advisory repeat creates another required disposition. This preserves existing
conditional-lifetime explanations while identifying the precise required review.

Successor generation, schema/program/actor ordering, argument tuples and stable
priority ties are deterministic for fixed inputs. Time/work cuts remain explicit.
Root generated populations are visited before derived creators mutate their
status/eligibility. This is a validated generated initial fixture, with the same
explicit GeneratedFixtureTrace origin; no root rows are invented terminal states.
The additional pre-transition pass is full-test-only, shares the campaign's
observer ceilings, and never runs recursively or increases check's budget.

SEC7 design review provenance: [verbatim reviews](./reviews/3000.036.004-sec7-review-record.md).

Implementation-review clarifications: upload suppression intersects the positive
non-null requirements of every creating-write context, rather than borrowing one
branch's guards. Recipient probes distinguish the original viewer from the stored
recipient, verify the roster principal column and retain only its literal role
requirement for current-role comparison. Result identity adds to existing source
lineage; it never replaces copied protected-column provenance. Role-wide-write
suppression requires a structural positive viewer-to-target relationship equality.
Directed search includes mutable role/boolean/relationship dependencies, uses two
prioritized actors per tuple (others remain in breadth), and immediately drops
displaced beam worlds. The retained-world high-water mark and shared directed
attempt counter are reported. Disposition writers serialize read-modify-write
through a kernel file lock in ignored generated output, with automatic release
on exit. These are precision/resource corrections within the reviewed contract.

Corpus calibration additionally narrows the retained-role gate to reader probes
whose declared key binds the stored recipient principal to viewer. A parent row
looked up by resource id with an additional owner/customer/parent equality is an
ownership relationship, not this assignment-key gate. The old declared-intent
principal-branch advisory is removed for the same reason; broad retained-grant
advice remains conditional. On wave22 this removes the owner-row questions from
001/009/041 while retaining 021's driver assignment. Directed argument selection
varies bool/enum decision arguments independently on resource identity tuples;
noise text cannot correlate a resource id with just the false branch. Successor
selection keeps distinct priority resource states, not the first two audit-actor
variants. Recipient bindings include implicit upsert-key columns, and effective
column read branches are checked with their row and owner gates. Directed
argument grouping and role-class actor deduplication each record uncovered
alternatives even when their subsequent numeric caps are not reached. The
empty-world directed pass defers population to ordinary generated
initialization/breadth. All omitted populations and variants remain uncovered.

### 16.7 Reviewed optionality in the stock social template

The existing social template intentionally permits text messages without photos.
Ship its single pre-reviewed source/analyzer-bound optional-photo disposition as
ordinary template data, with that explicit reason. This uses the same per-note
file and invalidation as user reviews, never a detector exemption or automatic
acknowledgment of arbitrary notes. The record names only that exact diagnostic
and schema; template/analyzer changes invalidate it and fail starter qualification
until reviewed again. Init beside an existing authored schema does not install
this stock-source disposition. The template README explains the decision. Packed
starter qualification must run init/check unchanged and verify the record becomes
stale after a source edit. No social API or runtime authority changes.

### 16.8 Typed upsert key binding

An unrelated unique index of the same arity must not erase a retained-recipient
grant question. The compiler already resolves positional keys by their types;
the security detector must consume that selected write-target index, not repeat
an arity-only approximation. Cache compiled write-target read-site indexes by
canonical source site and table once per source analysis. When they identify one
index, align the source write's named/positional key expressions with that bound
index's columns and merge the implicit stored fields with its explicit payload.
Keep the existing source pin resolver as a fallback for unambiguous source forms.
No planner/runtime rule or authorization changes: this repairs advice/gate recall
for the already documented shape. A two-unique-index recipient-key regression
and a reader that rechecks the role pin the gate and its suppression. Re-measure
the corpus for any newly recognized gates and report their individual review cost.

### 16.9 Day-two precision and destructive evidence questions

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; commissioned by the wave-23
day-two addendum. This supersedes the upload shape and stock-template disposition
in §§16.2/16.5/16.7 and narrows stale-creation advice. It changes analysis and
finite witness selection, never runtime admission or application authority.

**Optional uploads.** Optional arguments and optional columns are a valid model,
including deliberate v2 loosening and exceptions such as a lockout without proof.
Do not gate them merely because their names suggest evidence. Gate an optional
image/file argument written directly to an optional creating media column only
when a positive conjunct of an authored `table always` invariant requires that
exact column to be non-null. Flatten conjunctions and recognize
`field != null` and the exactly equivalent `not (field = null)` in either operand
order. Other negation, an OR, an unrelated column, comment, product document or unknown predicate is not
a mandatory-media declaration. No natural-language specification parsing, deploy
diff exemption, lane exemption or new intent language is introduced. Required
child media with optional arguments does not, by itself, require a child row.
A child-local non-null branch is valid optional attachment creation, including
040's return photo, and cannot fail check on that basis. Gate missing parent
evidence only when an independent parent `always exists child[...]` conjunct
pins the exact parent-reference relationship and the operation creates that parent.
Such a child-only guard cannot satisfy the explicit parent-level obligation.
Match the exact created parent reference and every additional literal child-probe
filter (e.g. checkout vs return). Unknown filters do not establish this gate.
An unconditional ordinary sibling insertion satisfying that same probe discharges
the obligation; an unrelated optional attachment must not be gated for it.
Conditional or if-absent sibling insertions cannot establish that discharge.
Without that obligation, parent-plus-conditional-child creation may ask the existing
advisory optional-evidence question, but never imposes an acknowledgment.
 Existing dominating non-null
guards across every creating context still suppress the question. Required scalar media (or a required nonempty list of required media)
with required arguments needs no optional-upload review. A required list allowing
zero elements is not mandatory evidence. Existing generic optional
evidence advice remains advisory. Bump the analyzer revision; remove the stock
social disposition and its special injection/custody code, because intended
text-only messages now check without any acknowledgment, including after edits.
Keep individual source-bound reviews for the two genuine gated shapes.

**Stale eligibility.** Match a creator's dominating condition on a non-role,
non-immutable column that another authored mutation actually changes (or an
existence condition on a deletable dependency). A mere read of an immutable owner
or an unrelated write to that table is not mutable eligibility. A later operation
qualifies only when it writes an approval-like literal enum state (`approved`,
`accepted`, `authorized`, `granted`, `issued`, `active`, `confirmed`, `paid`,
`signed`) or a true approval/authorization flag, and updates the created table
or creates a directly referencing child. An arbitrary edit, inspection, rejection,
cleanup or status-copy is outside this name-based advisory fragment. Rechecking
the relevant eligibility column (or named existence dependency) suppresses that
path; merely reading an immutable owner or a different column cannot suppress it. Group all creator and later-write
sites into one question per created table and mutable dependency; use the
lexicographically least (filename, numeric line, numeric column) decision site,
with sorted creator/decision names and sites plus the eligibility columns. This is a question, not an assertion that
every such decision must repeat the check; unsupported forms stay uncovered.

**Create/edit asymmetry.** Add `N_CREATE_EDIT_ASYMMETRY`, ranked with likely-hole
questions (priority 2), and `N_PRIVATE_CREATE_EDIT_ASYMMETRY` at priority 1
for a write-only private payload. Compare
argument-supplied creation fields with actual authored updates to that field.
Generic table-wide differences remain ordinary insert inventory; the new classes
require a recovered column-specific conditional guard. Besides stable global
requirements and table update gates, recover a conditional
column guard of the form `if newValue != target.column: require authority`
(or the equivalent equality/else form). Resolve lexical aliases and helper
arguments. Match the exact table, selected target keys, column and assigned
expression; a guard for a different row/field cannot become this column's edit
guard. Only guaranteed leading requirements of the changed branch count. Mixed
conditions, unknown effects and guards separated by intervening writes do not
establish this match. This extraction is isolated to advice; it is not a proof
rule or a new runtime check. Emit only a definite failed implication from creation
admission to the stable edit guard; unknown/capped comparisons do not establish
asymmetry. Union the necessary restrictions of every resolved edit path for the same
editor/table/column, including paths without a recovered conditional guard; only
a definite failed implication to that union can emit. Group by creator/table/
column/guard, name the editor and exact guard.
Only after emitting the specific note, suppress the older generic insert-inventory
note for that creator/table/column; failed recovery must retain the generic signal. It cannot
obscure the more specific question; defaults/literals remain quiet. A private
write-only escalation requires a definite failed implication from creator
admission to an explicit column-read gate, not merely an unknown read audience.
The message names that audience and asks whether this caller should initialize
the agency's private field. Both forms remain advisory and never infer the spec.

**Non-null witnesses.** Within the existing finite argument and campaign budgets,
prioritize non-null values for optional arguments directly supplying a declared
column-write intent before the null baseline. Preserve null alternatives and
explicit truncation notices. Only actual admitted writes judged against independent
intent refute; finite failure to find a witness remains unproved. Pin a non-null
042 billing-note witness for an added independent `agencyBillingNotes write only
owner` invariant, with actual GeneratedFixtureTrace/AuthoredFixtureTrace admission.
Its original read/operation intents alone do not assert this missing column policy.
Do not change its runtime rule or fabricate a row.

**Evidence removal.** Add advisory priority-2 `N_EVIDENCE_REMOVAL` for a media child
linked to a parent where the creation operation supplies required media and also
creates the parent with that exact reference bound to the created row id (mere
same-operation co-occurrence is insufficient), or independent parent `always exists child[...]` intent names
that relationship (the existing spelling is pinned by wave22/040-spec.q). Identify authored deletes, changes to the parent-reference
column, and explicit null/empty writes to evidence constrained non-null/nonempty
by independent intent. Group by child/parent-reference and list the media fields
and removing operations. Required media types alone do not prove the parent needs
at least one child; phrase this distinction explicitly. Ask whether the last
required evidence row can be removed, and point to a transactional last-child
guard, replacement, parent retirement, and independent integrity intent plus test.
Ordinary replacements of one non-null image are not removals. An optional
attachment with neither required-creation structure nor independent integrity
intent is outside the detector. A source guard may make removal intentional;
this is reviewable advice, not a gate or a claim that the guard is absent.

Qualification pins the twelve wave-23 loosen lanes 003,007,011,015,019,023,027,
031,035,039,043,047 (including 003's conditional
exception) with zero optional-upload gates; retains explicit parent-required-child and direct non-null intent positive controls;
040 return attachments are a separate negative control; pins a single focused 030 eligibility question,
042's owner-only billing guard and non-null witness, and 028's removal question.
Run corpus measurements before/after, report gate and advice counts separately,
and requalify the packed security guide/starters. Mac verification uses the named
Studio worktree only; SSH unavailability is reported, never counted as a pass.

Day-two fragment/qualification clarifications: these source heuristics are not
proof-completeness analyses. Nonmatching approval synonyms (`verified`, `cleared`,
`vetted`), mixed change conditions and early-return equivalents do not establish
safety and do not generate one uncovered record per syntactic nonmatch. Their
absence is documented here and in the guide; generic inventory and explicit
intent remain available. Pin different-row, mixed-condition, intervening-write
and early-return fallbacks, fee quietness, optional attachment exclusion, unrelated
co-insert exclusion and ordinary replacement controls. Measure all corpus check
latencies against the existing five-second authoring target; no new unbounded
search or larger kernel implication budget is introduced. The same static
creator/column/guard worklist uses the kernel's existing bounded implication.
Stock social qualification now asserts no shipped reviews file, no upload gate
and check exit0 before and after edits. Analyzer sec7-6 rejects sec7-2 through sec7-5 records.

Implementation review repairs: sibling discharge is restricted to a direct ordinary
insert in the mutation's straight-line prefix before any conditional/loop/as/return
control flow, not absence of a branch marker. Conditional expressions, zero-iteration
loops, helpers and if-absent inserts cannot establish that discharge. Stale-check
suppression requires every eligible column on the same checked entity, identified
by its unique key after mapping creator arguments/viewer through immutable stored
fields to the particular later target row or its child reference. A recheck of the
inspector instead of the applicant, another resource, or only one of two columns
cannot suppress. Ambiguous identity retains an advisory question and never suppresses.
Changed-field extraction treats remaining calls/writes in Require and If conditions,
branch requirements and compound expressions as effects; pending facts are discarded
before an effectful condition or use, and cannot cross a write or unknown call.
Fresh helper-local column facts may still be recovered after argument resolution.
These are conservative source-analysis fragments, not a new proof of stability.

Identity normalization is deliberately closed: only literal keys and resolved
immutable created-row captures may establish cross-operation identity. Unmapped
names (`viewer`, arguments), arbitrary reads/calls and other printed expressions
cannot compare equal merely because the same spelling appears in both operations.
A later-read capture field must itself be immutable; sharing an initial value
with a different immutable field does not establish enduring identity. Unknown
creator identity retains the question; unknown decision identity cannot suppress.
The final analyzer revision is sec7-7, invalidating earlier in-progress revisions.

Effect emission also ends pending column facts before evaluating its payload or
once-key, and those expressions are walked for authored writes. Qualification
pins an emit-hidden write fallback, unknown parent-probe filters, exact-parent
mismatch, two independent eligibility groups and two evidence reference groups.
An analyzer upgrade may require existing source-bound reviews to be revisited;
it never silently carries an earlier revision's acknowledgment forward.

Final closed-fragment repairs: named-id selectors must be literal IDs or typed
reference arguments (including `.id` of a read already pinned by those selectors),
never printed mutable lookups. A creator capture is compared only through literal
or immutable operation-input/viewer values, not separately evaluated calls/reads.
A decision that creates/deletes/upserts the captured source table cannot establish
capture stability. Branch/loop assignments poison affected advisory aliases at the
join, including argument shadowing; branch bindings cannot establish a new change
guard by retaining an old environment. Inherited column facts never enter a loop
or survive its domain evaluation. A column guard whose dependencies the editor can
write is not treated as stable. These conservative cuts leave ordinary inventory
and independent replay available, without claiming nonmatching source is safe.

An exact `argument ! else CODE` value enforces non-null at the creating write.
Without independent parent evidence intent this is valid conditional evidence,
not an upload gate (the wave18/19 respirator pass-card controls). With an explicit
parent obligation, an asserted child can discharge it only through the same direct
unconditional-creation fragment; an assertion inside a skipped branch does not
require a child. Omission aborting the atomic mutation cannot commit an evidence-
less parent. Assert-wrapped subexpressions inside a nullable alternative are not
assumed to make the entire field non-null.

To avoid confusing an old captured row with an intervening version, the specific
column class requires exactly one resolved write to that editor's target table,
and literal or immutable operation-input/viewer target keys. Multiple writes or
mutable key expressions keep ordinary inventory. The compiled editor write set
must not intersect recovered authority dependencies. Module-local editors and
creator argument flow remain distinct across source files. Conditional and loop
alias invalidation also applies when the advisory walker expands helper arguments.

Primary-row capture uses compiler-selected read/write-target index metadata cached
by source site and table, never a typed reference passed to a named non-ID lookup.
Explicit non-ID keys stay distinct even when their value has the table's reference
type; unknown or ambiguous selected indexes cannot establish a row-ID capture.
Upload deduplication uses the qualified module/operation, argument, table, column
and source site, and final
diagnostic deduplication preserves source sites, so distinct operations retain
separate individual review IDs even for the same media column.

Final precision boundary: a required media column is already validated at every
executed write; optional argument syntax alone cannot bypass that row contract.
Without independent parent necessity, do not gate an already-protected required
column. Direct nullable argument writes to an optional media column with explicit
non-null intent remain gated; unknown/derived helper returns or nullable expression
alternatives stay in advisory/independent-test coverage, never guessed argument-
omission failures. A non-null collection intent does not require an image when
its declared list allows zero elements. Explicit parent-required-child omission
retains the separate conditional-creation gate, even when the child value itself
is asserted, because a skipped child performs no assertion.

Non-null column-write argument prioritization excludes optional row references and
principals, preserving nullable relationship setup histories such as unclaimed
children. The existing relationship/write-intent regression pins that frontier.

Final precision cuts after independent review: the parent upload gate requires an
actual nullable-argument branch that can skip child creation; an unconditional
child validated through a helper is never guessed to omit evidence. Unknown
helper/control-flow cases remain advisory/testing. Eligibility suppression across
a child-to-parent capture requires exactly one resolved parent identity; multiple
or unknown parent references keep the question. Upload review obligations retain
operation, argument, table, column and source site, including two sites in one
mutation. Analyzer sec7-7 invalidates in-progress revisions2 through6.
